^SAMPLE = GSM8241199
!Sample_title = 91.WT.0h
!Sample_geo_accession = GSM8241199
!Sample_status = Public on Sep 13 2024
!Sample_submission_date = Apr 29 2024
!Sample_last_update_date = Sep 13 2024
!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Homo sapiens
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 9606
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cell type: CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: CD226-307Gly
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cd226 allele: rs736631C
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = treatment: anti-CD3/anti-CD28 mAbs
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = time: 0h
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = FACS-sorted CD3+CD8+ T cells from healthy donors homozygous for either the protective (CD226-307Gly, N = 10) or risk allele (CD226-307Ser, N = 10) cultured in complete medium RPMI and stimulated with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 monoclonal antibodies for 0h, 4h and 24h were subjected to RNA extraction using RNeasy Micro Kit. Paired-end (2x150pb) sequencing libraries were prepared using  Illumina’s Stranded Total RNA prep kit starting from 7,5ng of total RNA using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. Sequencing libraries were indexed using IDT Illumina UDI A Lig 96 indexes 96 Sp1. RNA sequencing was performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina Novaseq 6000 sequencer using Novaseq 6000 S4 1v5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles) yielding between 20 and 40 millions raw paired-end reads per library.
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Peripheral blood mononuclear leukocytes (PBMCs) were purified from buffy coat preparations obtained from consenting healthy blood donors. PBMCs were prepared by gradient centrifugation of buffy coats and kept in frozen in liquid nitrogen. Genotyping of the CD226 SNP rs763361 was performed using TaqMan SNP Genotyping assay. rs763361T expressing CD226-307Ser represented the risk allele, whereas rs763361C expressing CD226-307Gly represented the protective allele. Experiments were performed on cells form male donors homozygous for either allele.Frozen PBMCs were thawed, monocytes removed by plastic adherence and CD8 T cells were purified by negative selection. The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = RNA was extracted using RNAeasy Micro Kit (Qiagen) following manufacturer’s instructions.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Libraries were prepared using the Illumina Stranded Total RNA Prep kit starting from 7.5 ng of total RNA and using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. The kit for the index sequences used was IDT Illumina RNA UDI A Lig 96 Indexes 96 Spl. RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.
!Sample_description = WT.0h.91
!Sample_description = raw_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = DESeq2_normalized_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = TPMs.tsv
!Sample_description = SNP.0h.vs.WT.0h.tsv
!Sample_description = WT.4h.vs.WT.0h.tsv
!Sample_description = WT.12h.vs.WT.0h.tsv
!Sample_description = WT.24h.vs.WT.0h.tsv
!Sample_description = samples.RDS
!Sample_description = salmon_tx2gene.RDS
!Sample_description = hsapiens_gene_ensembl_v104.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_unfiltered_60664_genes.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_filtered_14965_genes.RDS
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were processed using nf-core/rnaseq pipeline (v3.8.1) on genotoul SLURM cluster using -profile genotoul, --fasta Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.dna.primary_assembly.fa, --gtf Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.104.gtf ,-- aligner star_salmon options.
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were quality controlled using FASTQC (v0.11.9)
!Sample_data_processing = Adapter and quality trimming was performed using Trim Galore! (v3.4)
!Sample_data_processing = Trimmed reads were aligned to reference genome using STAR (v2.7.10a), BAM files were sorted and indexed using samtools (v1.15.1), duplicates were marked using picard MarkDuplicates (v 2.26.10)
!Sample_data_processing = Pseudo-alignment and quantification were performed using salmon (v 1.5.2)
!Sample_data_processing = Salmon transcripts quantification files (quant.sf) were imported into R/Rstudio using R ‘tximport’ package and a file linking Transcript ID to Gene ID (‘tx2gene’ file) generated by the nf-core/rnaseq pipeline to produce the raw counts matrix. TPMs values were retrieved from the abundance slot of tximport generated list.
!Sample_data_processing = For differential gene expression analyses, the raw count matrix was converted to a DESeq2 object using ‘DESeq2::DESeqDataSetFromTximport’  function with design : ~ group, where the group represent the combination of Genotype (i.e. WT for rs736631C/CD226-307Gly protective allele or SNP for rs736631T/CD226-307Ser risk allele) and treatment time (i.e. WT.0h, SNP.0h, WT.4h, SNP.4H, WT.12h, SNP.12h, WT.24h, SNP.24h).
!Sample_data_processing = Raw count matrix was further processed to remove genes that have low / inconsistent gene expression level in samples from the same ‘Group’ (i.e. WT.0h, WT.4h, WT.12h, WT.24h,  SNP.0h, SNP.4h, SNP.12h, SNP.24h). Raw counts were normalized using DESeq2::counts function and genes having less than 10 normalized counts in all groups in 2 of the 10 samples from the same group were filtered out yielding a normalized count matrix with 14965 genes.
!Sample_data_processing = Pairwise differential gene expression analyses were performed to compare SNP.0h vs WT.0h; SNP.4h vs WT.4h; SNP.12h vs WT.12h; SNP.24h vs WT.24h; SNP.4h vs SNP.0h; SNP.12h vs SNP.0h; SNP.24h vs SNP.0h; WT.4h vs WT.0h; WT.12h vs WT.0h and WT.24h vs WT.0h groups using DESeq2::results function with options lfcThreshold set to 0 and alpha set to 0.05.
!Sample_data_processing = For each pairwise comparison, DESeq2 results were exported as a tab-delimited file with raw counts, normalized counts and TPMs for all samples being compared.
!Sample_data_processing = Assembly: ENSEMBL GRCh38 (hg38) v104
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes raw counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes DESeq2 normalized counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes Transcript-per-Million (TPMs) counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding sample information
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding Transcript ID to GeneID mapping info (i.e. tx2gene file used to import salmon quantification file in R using Tximport)
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file for raw count matrix
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file for DESeq2 filtered normalized counts matrix
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding Ensembl gene mapping information for annotation of genes in count matrices

!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were quality controlled using FASTQC (v0.11.9)
!Sample_data_processing = Adapter and quality trimming was performed using Trim Galore! (v3.4)
!Sample_data_processing = Trimmed reads were aligned to reference genome using STAR (v2.7.10a), BAM files were sorted and indexed using samtools (v1.15.1), duplicates were marked using picard MarkDuplicates (v 2.26.10)
!Sample_data_processing = Pseudo-alignment and quantification were performed using salmon (v 1.5.2)
!Sample_data_processing = Salmon transcripts quantification files (quant.sf) were imported into R/Rstudio using R ‘tximport’ package and a file linking Transcript ID to Gene ID (‘tx2gene’ file) generated by the nf-core/rnaseq pipeline to produce the raw counts matrix. TPMs values were retrieved from the abundance slot of tximport generated list.
!Sample_data_processing = For differential gene expression analyses, the raw count matrix was converted to a DESeq2 object using ‘DESeq2::DESeqDataSetFromTximport’  function with design : ~ group, where the group represent the combination of Genotype (i.e. WT for rs736631C/CD226-307Gly protective allele or SNP for rs736631T/CD226-307Ser risk allele) and treatment time (i.e. WT.0h, SNP.0h, WT.4h, SNP.4H, WT.12h, SNP.12h, WT.24h, SNP.24h).
!Sample_data_processing = Raw count matrix was further processed to remove genes that have low / inconsistent gene expression level in samples from the same ‘Group’ (i.e. WT.0h, WT.4h, WT.12h, WT.24h,  SNP.0h, SNP.4h, SNP.12h, SNP.24h). Raw counts were normalized using DESeq2::counts function and genes having less than 10 normalized counts in all groups in 2 of the 10 samples from the same group were filtered out yielding a normalized count matrix with 14965 genes.
!Sample_data_processing = Pairwise differential gene expression analyses were performed to compare SNP.0h vs WT.0h; SNP.4h vs WT.4h; SNP.12h vs WT.12h; SNP.24h vs WT.24h; SNP.4h vs SNP.0h; SNP.12h vs SNP.0h; SNP.24h vs SNP.0h; WT.4h vs WT.0h; WT.12h vs WT.0h and WT.24h vs WT.0h groups using DESeq2::results function with options lfcThreshold set to 0 and alpha set to 0.05.
!Sample_data_processing = For each pairwise comparison, DESeq2 results were exported as a tab-delimited file with raw counts, normalized counts and TPMs for all samples being compared.

!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Homo sapiens
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 9606
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cell type: CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: CD226-307Gly
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cd226 allele: rs736631C
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = treatment: anti-CD3/anti-CD28 mAbs
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = time: 0h
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = FACS-sorted CD3+CD8+ T cells from healthy donors homozygous for either the protective (CD226-307Gly, N = 10) or risk allele (CD226-307Ser, N = 10) cultured in complete medium RPMI and stimulated with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 monoclonal antibodies for 0h, 4h and 24h were subjected to RNA extraction using RNeasy Micro Kit. Paired-end (2x150pb) sequencing libraries were prepared using  Illumina’s Stranded Total RNA prep kit starting from 7,5ng of total RNA using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. Sequencing libraries were indexed using IDT Illumina UDI A Lig 96 indexes 96 Sp1. RNA sequencing was performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina Novaseq 6000 sequencer using Novaseq 6000 S4 1v5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles) yielding between 20 and 40 millions raw paired-end reads per library.
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Peripheral blood mononuclear leukocytes (PBMCs) were purified from buffy coat preparations obtained from consenting healthy blood donors. PBMCs were prepared by gradient centrifugation of buffy coats and kept in frozen in liquid nitrogen. Genotyping of the CD226 SNP rs763361 was performed using TaqMan SNP Genotyping assay. rs763361T expressing CD226-307Ser represented the risk allele, whereas rs763361C expressing CD226-307Gly represented the protective allele. Experiments were performed on cells form male donors homozygous for either allele.Frozen PBMCs were thawed, monocytes removed by plastic adherence and CD8 T cells were purified by negative selection. The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = RNA was extracted using RNAeasy Micro Kit (Qiagen) following manufacturer’s instructions.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Libraries were prepared using the Illumina Stranded Total RNA Prep kit starting from 7.5 ng of total RNA and using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. The kit for the index sequences used was IDT Illumina RNA UDI A Lig 96 Indexes 96 Spl. RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.

Raw counts were normalized using DESeq2::counts function and genes having less than 10 normalized counts in all groups in 2 of the 10 samples from the same group were filtered out yielding a normalized count matrix with 14965 genes.
!Sample_data_processing = Pairwise differential gene expression analyses were performed to compare SNP.0h vs WT.0h; SNP.4h vs WT.4h; SNP.12h vs WT.12h; SNP.24h vs WT.24h; SNP.4h vs SNP.0h; SNP.12h vs SNP.0h; SNP.24h vs SNP.0h; WT.4h vs WT.0h; WT.12h vs WT.0h and WT.24h vs WT.0h groups using DESeq2::results function with options lfcThreshold set to 0 and alpha set to 0.05.
!Sample_data_processing = For each pairwise comparison, DESeq2 results were exported as a tab-delimited file with raw counts, normalized counts and TPMs for all samples being compared.
!Sample_data_processing = Assembly: ENSEMBL GRCh38 (hg38) v104
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes raw counts for each sample

!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Homo sapiens
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 9606
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cell type: CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: CD226-307Gly
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cd226 allele: rs736631C
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = treatment: anti-CD3/anti-CD28 mAbs
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = time: 0h
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = FACS-sorted CD3+CD8+ T cells from healthy donors homozygous for either the protective (CD226-307Gly, N = 10) or risk allele (CD226-307Ser, N = 10) cultured in complete medium RPMI and stimulated with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 monoclonal antibodies for 0h, 4h and 24h were subjected to RNA extraction using RNeasy Micro Kit. Paired-end (2x150pb) sequencing libraries were prepared using  Illumina’s Stranded Total RNA prep kit starting from 7,5ng of total RNA using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. Sequencing libraries were indexed using IDT Illumina UDI A Lig 96 indexes 96 Sp1. RNA sequencing was performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina Novaseq 6000 sequencer using Novaseq 6000 S4 1v5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles) yielding between 20 and 40 millions raw paired-end reads per library.
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Peripheral blood mononuclear leukocytes (PBMCs) were purified from buffy coat preparations obtained from consenting healthy blood donors. PBMCs were prepared by gradient centrifugation of buffy coats and kept in frozen in liquid nitrogen. Genotyping of the CD226 SNP rs763361 was performed using TaqMan SNP Genotyping assay. rs763361T expressing CD226-307Ser represented the risk allele, whereas rs763361C expressing CD226-307Gly represented the protective allele. Experiments were performed on cells form male donors homozygous for either allele.Frozen PBMCs were thawed, monocytes removed by plastic adherence and CD8 T cells were purified by negative selection. The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = RNA was extracted using RNAeasy Micro Kit (Qiagen) following manufacturer’s instructions.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Libraries were prepared using the Illumina Stranded Total RNA Prep kit starting from 7.5 ng of total RNA and using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. The kit for the index sequences used was IDT Illumina RNA UDI A Lig 96 Indexes 96 Spl. RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.

!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were processed using nf-core/rnaseq pipeline (v3.8.1) on genotoul SLURM cluster using -profile genotoul, --fasta Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.dna.primary_assembly.fa, --gtf Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.104.gtf ,-- aligner star_salmon options.
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were quality controlled using FASTQC (v0.11.9)
!Sample_data_processing = Adapter and quality trimming was performed using Trim Galore! (v3.4)
!Sample_data_processing = Trimmed reads were aligned to reference genome using STAR (v2.7.10a), BAM files were sorted and indexed using samtools (v1.15.1), duplicates were marked using picard MarkDuplicates (v 2.26.10)
!Sample_data_processing = Pseudo-alignment and quantification were performed using salmon (v 1.5.2)
!Sample_data_processing = Salmon transcripts quantification files (quant.sf) were imported into R/Rstudio using R ‘tximport’ package and a file linking Transcript ID to Gene ID (‘tx2gene’ file) generated by the nf-core/rnaseq pipeline to produce the raw counts matrix. TPMs values were retrieved from the abundance slot of tximport generated list.

!Sample_contact_city = Toulouse
!Sample_contact_zip/postal_code = 31024
!Sample_contact_country = France
!Sample_instrument_model = Illumina NovaSeq 6000
!Sample_library_selection = cDNA
!Sample_library_source = transcriptomic
!Sample_library_strategy = RNA-Seq
!Sample_relation = BioSample: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/biosample/SAMN41114963
!Sample_relation = SRA: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sra?term=SRX24396685
!Sample_supplementary_file_1 = NONE
!Sample_series_id = GSE266132
!Sample_data_row_count = 0
^SAMPLE = GSM8241209
!Sample_title = 101.SNP.0h
!Sample_geo_accession = GSM8241209
!Sample_status = Public on Sep 13 2024
!Sample_submission_date = Apr 29 2024
!Sample_last_update_date = Sep 13 2024
!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Homo sapiens
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 9606
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cell type: CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: CD226-307Ser
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cd226 allele: rs736631T
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = treatment: anti-CD3/anti-CD28 mAbs
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = time: 0h
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = FACS-sorted CD3+CD8+ T cells from healthy donors homozygous for either the protective (CD226-307Gly, N = 10) or risk allele (CD226-307Ser, N = 10) cultured in complete medium RPMI and stimulated with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 monoclonal antibodies for 0h, 4h and 24h were subjected to RNA extraction using RNeasy Micro Kit. Paired-end (2x150pb) sequencing libraries were prepared using  Illumina’s Stranded Total RNA prep kit starting from 7,5ng of total RNA using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. Sequencing libraries were indexed using IDT Illumina UDI A Lig 96 indexes 96 Sp1. RNA sequencing was performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina Novaseq 6000 sequencer using Novaseq 6000 S4 1v5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles) yielding between 20 and 40 millions raw paired-end reads per library.
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Peripheral blood mononuclear leukocytes (PBMCs) were purified from buffy coat preparations obtained from consenting healthy blood donors. PBMCs were prepared by gradient centrifugation of buffy coats and kept in frozen in liquid nitrogen. Genotyping of the CD226 SNP rs763361 was performed using TaqMan SNP Genotyping assay. rs763361T expressing CD226-307Ser represented the risk allele, whereas rs763361C expressing CD226-307Gly represented the protective allele. Experiments were performed on cells form male donors homozygous for either allele.Frozen PBMCs were thawed, monocytes removed by plastic adherence and CD8 T cells were purified by negative selection. The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = RNA was extracted using RNAeasy Micro Kit (Qiagen) following manufacturer’s instructions.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Libraries were prepared using the Illumina Stranded Total RNA Prep kit starting from 7.5 ng of total RNA and using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. The kit for the index sequences used was IDT Illumina RNA UDI A Lig 96 Indexes 96 Spl. RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.
!Sample_description = SNP.0h.101
!Sample_description = raw_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = DESeq2_normalized_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = TPMs.tsv
!Sample_description = SNP.0h.vs.WT.0h.tsv
!Sample_description = SNP.4h.vs.SNP.0h.tsv
!Sample_description = SNP.12h.vs.SNP.0h.tsv
!Sample_description = SNP.24h.vs.SNP.0h.tsv
!Sample_description = samples.RDS
!Sample_description = salmon_tx2gene.RDS
!Sample_description = hsapiens_gene_ensembl_v104.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_unfiltered_60664_genes.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_filtered_14965_genes.RDS
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were processed using nf-core/rnaseq pipeline (v3.8.1) on genotoul SLURM cluster using -profile genotoul, --fasta Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.dna.primary_assembly.fa, --gtf Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.104.gtf ,-- aligner star_salmon options.
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were quality controlled using FASTQC (v0.11.9)
!Sample_data_processing = Adapter and quality trimming was performed using Trim Galore! (v3.4)
!Sample_data_processing = Trimmed reads were aligned to reference genome using STAR (v2.7.10a), BAM files were sorted and indexed using samtools (v1.15.1), duplicates were marked using picard MarkDuplicates (v 2.26.10)
!Sample_data_processing = Pseudo-alignment and quantification were performed using salmon (v 1.5.2)
!Sample_data_processing = Salmon transcripts quantification files (quant.sf) were imported into R/Rstudio using R ‘tximport’ package and a file linking Transcript ID to Gene ID (‘tx2gene’ file) generated by the nf-core/rnaseq pipeline to produce the raw counts matrix. TPMs values were retrieved from the abundance slot of tximport generated list.
!Sample_data_processing = For differential gene expression analyses, the raw count matrix was converted to a DESeq2 object using ‘DESeq2::DESeqDataSetFromTximport’  function with design : ~ group, where the group represent the combination of Genotype (i.e. WT for rs736631C/CD226-307Gly protective allele or SNP for rs736631T/CD226-307Ser risk allele) and treatment time (i.e. WT.0h, SNP.0h, WT.4h, SNP.4H, WT.12h, SNP.12h, WT.24h, SNP.24h).
!Sample_data_processing = Raw count matrix was further processed to remove genes that have low / inconsistent gene expression level in samples from the same ‘Group’ (i.e. WT.0h, WT.4h, WT.12h, WT.24h,  SNP.0h, SNP.4h, SNP.12h, SNP.24h). Raw counts were normalized using DESeq2::counts function and genes having less than 10 normalized counts in all groups in 2 of the 10 samples from the same group were filtered out yielding a normalized count matrix with 14965 genes.
!Sample_data_processing = Pairwise differential gene expression analyses were performed to compare SNP.0h vs WT.0h; SNP.4h vs WT.4h; SNP.12h vs WT.12h; SNP.24h vs WT.24h; SNP.4h vs SNP.0h; SNP.12h vs SNP.0h; SNP.24h vs SNP.0h; WT.4h vs WT.0h; WT.12h vs WT.0h and WT.24h vs WT.0h groups using DESeq2::results function with options lfcThreshold set to 0 and alpha set to 0.05.
!Sample_data_processing = For each pairwise comparison, DESeq2 results were exported as a tab-delimited file with raw counts, normalized counts and TPMs for all samples being compared.
!Sample_data_processing = Assembly: ENSEMBL GRCh38 (hg38) v104
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes raw counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes DESeq2 normalized counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes Transcript-per-Million (TPMs) counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding sample information
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding Transcript ID to GeneID mapping info (i.e. tx2gene file used to import salmon quantification file in R using Tximport)
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file for raw count matrix
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file for DESeq2 filtered normalized counts matrix
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding Ensembl gene mapping information for annotation of genes in count matrices

The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = RNA was extracted using RNAeasy Micro Kit (Qiagen) following manufacturer’s instructions.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Libraries were prepared using the Illumina Stranded Total RNA Prep kit starting from 7.5 ng of total RNA and using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. The kit for the index sequences used was IDT Illumina RNA UDI A Lig 96 Indexes 96 Spl. RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.

!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were processed using nf-core/rnaseq pipeline (v3.8.1) on genotoul SLURM cluster using -profile genotoul, --fasta Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.dna.primary_assembly.fa, --gtf Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.104.gtf ,-- aligner star_salmon options.
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were quality controlled using FASTQC (v0.11.9)
!Sample_data_processing = Adapter and quality trimming was performed using Trim Galore! (v3.4)
!Sample_data_processing = Trimmed reads were aligned to reference genome using STAR (v2.7.10a), BAM files were sorted and indexed using samtools (v1.15.1), duplicates were marked using picard MarkDuplicates (v 2.26.10)
!Sample_data_processing = Pseudo-alignment and quantification were performed using salmon (v 1.5.2)
!Sample_data_processing = Salmon transcripts quantification files (quant.sf) were imported into R/Rstudio using R ‘tximport’ package and a file linking Transcript ID to Gene ID (‘tx2gene’ file) generated by the nf-core/rnaseq pipeline to produce the raw counts matrix. TPMs values were retrieved from the abundance slot of tximport generated list.
!Sample_data_processing = For differential gene expression analyses, the raw count matrix was converted to a DESeq2 object using ‘DESeq2::DESeqDataSetFromTximport’  function with design : ~ group, where the group represent the combination of Genotype (i.e. WT for rs736631C/CD226-307Gly protective allele or SNP for rs736631T/CD226-307Ser risk allele) and treatment time (i.e. WT.0h, SNP.0h, WT.4h, SNP.4H, WT.12h, SNP.12h, WT.24h, SNP.24h).
!Sample_data_processing = Raw count matrix was further processed to remove genes that have low / inconsistent gene expression level in samples from the same ‘Group’ (i.e. WT.0h, WT.4h, WT.12h, WT.24h,  SNP.0h, SNP.4h, SNP.12h, SNP.24h). Raw counts were normalized using DESeq2::counts function and genes having less than 10 normalized counts in all groups in 2 of the 10 samples from the same group were filtered out yielding a normalized count matrix with 14965 genes.
!Sample_data_processing = Pairwise differential gene expression analyses were performed to compare SNP.0h vs WT.0h; SNP.4h vs WT.4h; SNP.12h vs WT.12h; SNP.24h vs WT.24h; SNP.4h vs SNP.0h; SNP.12h vs SNP.0h; SNP.24h vs SNP.0h; WT.4h vs WT.0h; WT.12h vs WT.0h and WT.24h vs WT.0h groups using DESeq2::results function with options lfcThreshold set to 0 and alpha set to 0.05.
!Sample_data_processing = For each pairwise comparison, DESeq2 results were exported as a tab-delimited file with raw counts, normalized counts and TPMs for all samples being compared.

!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = FACS-sorted CD3+CD8+ T cells from healthy donors homozygous for either the protective (CD226-307Gly, N = 10) or risk allele (CD226-307Ser, N = 10) cultured in complete medium RPMI and stimulated with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 monoclonal antibodies for 0h, 4h and 24h were subjected to RNA extraction using RNeasy Micro Kit. Paired-end (2x150pb) sequencing libraries were prepared using  Illumina’s Stranded Total RNA prep kit starting from 7,5ng of total RNA using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. Sequencing libraries were indexed using IDT Illumina UDI A Lig 96 indexes 96 Sp1. RNA sequencing was performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina Novaseq 6000 sequencer using Novaseq 6000 S4 1v5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles) yielding between 20 and 40 millions raw paired-end reads per library.
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Peripheral blood mononuclear leukocytes (PBMCs) were purified from buffy coat preparations obtained from consenting healthy blood donors. PBMCs were prepared by gradient centrifugation of buffy coats and kept in frozen in liquid nitrogen. Genotyping of the CD226 SNP rs763361 was performed using TaqMan SNP Genotyping assay. rs763361T expressing CD226-307Ser represented the risk allele, whereas rs763361C expressing CD226-307Gly represented the protective allele. Experiments were performed on cells form male donors homozygous for either allele.Frozen PBMCs were thawed, monocytes removed by plastic adherence and CD8 T cells were purified by negative selection. The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.

RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.

!Sample_data_processing = Salmon transcripts quantification files (quant.sf) were imported into R/Rstudio using R ‘tximport’ package and a file linking Transcript ID to Gene ID (‘tx2gene’ file) generated by the nf-core/rnaseq pipeline to produce the raw counts matrix. TPMs values were retrieved from the abundance slot of tximport generated list.
!Sample_data_processing = For differential gene expression analyses, the raw count matrix was converted to a DESeq2 object using ‘DESeq2::DESeqDataSetFromTximport’  function with design : ~ group, where the group represent the combination of Genotype (i.e. WT for rs736631C/CD226-307Gly protective allele or SNP for rs736631T/CD226-307Ser risk allele) and treatment time (i.e. WT.0h, SNP.0h, WT.4h, SNP.4H, WT.12h, SNP.12h, WT.24h, SNP.24h).
!Sample_data_processing = Raw count matrix was further processed to remove genes that have low / inconsistent gene expression level in samples from the same ‘Group’ (i.e. WT.0h, WT.4h, WT.12h, WT.24h,  SNP.0h, SNP.4h, SNP.12h, SNP.24h). Raw counts were normalized using DESeq2::counts function and genes having less than 10 normalized counts in all groups in 2 of the 10 samples from the same group were filtered out yielding a normalized count matrix with 14965 genes.
!Sample_data_processing = Pairwise differential gene expression analyses were performed to compare SNP.0h vs WT.0h; SNP.4h vs WT.4h; SNP.12h vs WT.12h; SNP.24h vs WT.24h; SNP.4h vs SNP.0h; SNP.12h vs SNP.0h; SNP.24h vs SNP.0h; WT.4h vs WT.0h; WT.12h vs WT.0h and WT.24h vs WT.0h groups using DESeq2::results function with options lfcThreshold set to 0 and alpha set to 0.05.
!Sample_data_processing = For each pairwise comparison, DESeq2 results were exported as a tab-delimited file with raw counts, normalized counts and TPMs for all samples being compared.
!Sample_data_processing = Assembly: ENSEMBL GRCh38 (hg38) v104

!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file for DESeq2 filtered normalized counts matrix

!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Homo sapiens
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 9606
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cell type: CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: CD226-307Ser
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cd226 allele: rs736631T
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = treatment: anti-CD3/anti-CD28 mAbs
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = time: 0h
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = FACS-sorted CD3+CD8+ T cells from healthy donors homozygous for either the protective (CD226-307Gly, N = 10) or risk allele (CD226-307Ser, N = 10) cultured in complete medium RPMI and stimulated with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 monoclonal antibodies for 0h, 4h and 24h were subjected to RNA extraction using RNeasy Micro Kit. Paired-end (2x150pb) sequencing libraries were prepared using  Illumina’s Stranded Total RNA prep kit starting from 7,5ng of total RNA using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. Sequencing libraries were indexed using IDT Illumina UDI A Lig 96 indexes 96 Sp1. RNA sequencing was performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina Novaseq 6000 sequencer using Novaseq 6000 S4 1v5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles) yielding between 20 and 40 millions raw paired-end reads per library.
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Peripheral blood mononuclear leukocytes (PBMCs) were purified from buffy coat preparations obtained from consenting healthy blood donors. PBMCs were prepared by gradient centrifugation of buffy coats and kept in frozen in liquid nitrogen. Genotyping of the CD226 SNP rs763361 was performed using TaqMan SNP Genotyping assay. rs763361T expressing CD226-307Ser represented the risk allele, whereas rs763361C expressing CD226-307Gly represented the protective allele. Experiments were performed on cells form male donors homozygous for either allele.Frozen PBMCs were thawed, monocytes removed by plastic adherence and CD8 T cells were purified by negative selection. The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = RNA was extracted using RNAeasy Micro Kit (Qiagen) following manufacturer’s instructions.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Libraries were prepared using the Illumina Stranded Total RNA Prep kit starting from 7.5 ng of total RNA and using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. The kit for the index sequences used was IDT Illumina RNA UDI A Lig 96 Indexes 96 Spl. RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.

^SAMPLE = GSM8241219
!Sample_title = 91.WT.4h
!Sample_geo_accession = GSM8241219
!Sample_status = Public on Sep 13 2024
!Sample_submission_date = Apr 29 2024
!Sample_last_update_date = Sep 13 2024
!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Homo sapiens
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 9606
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cell type: CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: CD226-307Gly
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cd226 allele: rs736631C
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = treatment: anti-CD3/anti-CD28 mAbs
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = time: 4h
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = FACS-sorted CD3+CD8+ T cells from healthy donors homozygous for either the protective (CD226-307Gly, N = 10) or risk allele (CD226-307Ser, N = 10) cultured in complete medium RPMI and stimulated with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 monoclonal antibodies for 0h, 4h and 24h were subjected to RNA extraction using RNeasy Micro Kit. Paired-end (2x150pb) sequencing libraries were prepared using  Illumina’s Stranded Total RNA prep kit starting from 7,5ng of total RNA using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. Sequencing libraries were indexed using IDT Illumina UDI A Lig 96 indexes 96 Sp1. RNA sequencing was performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina Novaseq 6000 sequencer using Novaseq 6000 S4 1v5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles) yielding between 20 and 40 millions raw paired-end reads per library.
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Peripheral blood mononuclear leukocytes (PBMCs) were purified from buffy coat preparations obtained from consenting healthy blood donors. PBMCs were prepared by gradient centrifugation of buffy coats and kept in frozen in liquid nitrogen. Genotyping of the CD226 SNP rs763361 was performed using TaqMan SNP Genotyping assay. rs763361T expressing CD226-307Ser represented the risk allele, whereas rs763361C expressing CD226-307Gly represented the protective allele. Experiments were performed on cells form male donors homozygous for either allele.Frozen PBMCs were thawed, monocytes removed by plastic adherence and CD8 T cells were purified by negative selection. The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = RNA was extracted using RNAeasy Micro Kit (Qiagen) following manufacturer’s instructions.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Libraries were prepared using the Illumina Stranded Total RNA Prep kit starting from 7.5 ng of total RNA and using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. The kit for the index sequences used was IDT Illumina RNA UDI A Lig 96 Indexes 96 Spl. RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.
!Sample_description = WT.4h.91
!Sample_description = raw_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = DESeq2_normalized_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = TPMs.tsv
!Sample_description = SNP.4h.vs.WT.4h.tsv
!Sample_description = WT.4h.vs.WT.0h.tsv
!Sample_description = samples.RDS
!Sample_description = salmon_tx2gene.RDS
!Sample_description = hsapiens_gene_ensembl_v104.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_unfiltered_60664_genes.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_filtered_14965_genes.RDS
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were processed using nf-core/rnaseq pipeline (v3.8.1) on genotoul SLURM cluster using -profile genotoul, --fasta Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.dna.primary_assembly.fa, --gtf Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.104.gtf ,-- aligner star_salmon options.
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were quality controlled using FASTQC (v0.11.9)
!Sample_data_processing = Adapter and quality trimming was performed using Trim Galore! (v3.4)
!Sample_data_processing = Trimmed reads were aligned to reference genome using STAR (v2.7.10a), BAM files were sorted and indexed using samtools (v1.15.1), duplicates were marked using picard MarkDuplicates (v 2.26.10)
!Sample_data_processing = Pseudo-alignment and quantification were performed using salmon (v 1.5.2)
!Sample_data_processing = Salmon transcripts quantification files (quant.sf) were imported into R/Rstudio using R ‘tximport’ package and a file linking Transcript ID to Gene ID (‘tx2gene’ file) generated by the nf-core/rnaseq pipeline to produce the raw counts matrix. TPMs values were retrieved from the abundance slot of tximport generated list.
!Sample_data_processing = For differential gene expression analyses, the raw count matrix was converted to a DESeq2 object using ‘DESeq2::DESeqDataSetFromTximport’  function with design : ~ group, where the group represent the combination of Genotype (i.e. WT for rs736631C/CD226-307Gly protective allele or SNP for rs736631T/CD226-307Ser risk allele) and treatment time (i.e. WT.0h, SNP.0h, WT.4h, SNP.4H, WT.12h, SNP.12h, WT.24h, SNP.24h).
!Sample_data_processing = Raw count matrix was further processed to remove genes that have low / inconsistent gene expression level in samples from the same ‘Group’ (i.e. WT.0h, WT.4h, WT.12h, WT.24h,  SNP.0h, SNP.4h, SNP.12h, SNP.24h). Raw counts were normalized using DESeq2::counts function and genes having less than 10 normalized counts in all groups in 2 of the 10 samples from the same group were filtered out yielding a normalized count matrix with 14965 genes.
!Sample_data_processing = Pairwise differential gene expression analyses were performed to compare SNP.0h vs WT.0h; SNP.4h vs WT.4h; SNP.12h vs WT.12h; SNP.24h vs WT.24h; SNP.4h vs SNP.0h; SNP.12h vs SNP.0h; SNP.24h vs SNP.0h; WT.4h vs WT.0h; WT.12h vs WT.0h and WT.24h vs WT.0h groups using DESeq2::results function with options lfcThreshold set to 0 and alpha set to 0.05.
!Sample_data_processing = For each pairwise comparison, DESeq2 results were exported as a tab-delimited file with raw counts, normalized counts and TPMs for all samples being compared.
!Sample_data_processing = Assembly: ENSEMBL GRCh38 (hg38) v104

Genotyping of the CD226 SNP rs763361 was performed using TaqMan SNP Genotyping assay. rs763361T expressing CD226-307Ser represented the risk allele, whereas rs763361C expressing CD226-307Gly represented the protective allele. Experiments were performed on cells form male donors homozygous for either allele.Frozen PBMCs were thawed, monocytes removed by plastic adherence and CD8 T cells were purified by negative selection. The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = RNA was extracted using RNAeasy Micro Kit (Qiagen) following manufacturer’s instructions.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Libraries were prepared using the Illumina Stranded Total RNA Prep kit starting from 7.5 ng of total RNA and using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. The kit for the index sequences used was IDT Illumina RNA UDI A Lig 96 Indexes 96 Spl. RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.
!Sample_description = WT.4h.167
!Sample_description = raw_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = DESeq2_normalized_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = TPMs.tsv
!Sample_description = SNP.4h.vs.WT.4h.tsv
!Sample_description = WT.4h.vs.WT.0h.tsv
!Sample_description = samples.RDS
!Sample_description = salmon_tx2gene.RDS
!Sample_description = hsapiens_gene_ensembl_v104.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_unfiltered_60664_genes.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_filtered_14965_genes.RDS
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were processed using nf-core/rnaseq pipeline (v3.8.1) on genotoul SLURM cluster using -profile genotoul, --fasta Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.dna.primary_assembly.fa, --gtf Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.104.gtf ,-- aligner star_salmon options.
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were quality controlled using FASTQC (v0.11.9)
!Sample_data_processing = Adapter and quality trimming was performed using Trim Galore! (v3.4)
!Sample_data_processing = Trimmed reads were aligned to reference genome using STAR (v2.7.10a), BAM files were sorted and indexed using samtools (v1.15.1), duplicates were marked using picard MarkDuplicates (v 2.26.10)
!Sample_data_processing = Pseudo-alignment and quantification were performed using salmon (v 1.5.2)
!Sample_data_processing = Salmon transcripts quantification files (quant.sf) were imported into R/Rstudio using R ‘tximport’ package and a file linking Transcript ID to Gene ID (‘tx2gene’ file) generated by the nf-core/rnaseq pipeline to produce the raw counts matrix. TPMs values were retrieved from the abundance slot of tximport generated list.
!Sample_data_processing = For differential gene expression analyses, the raw count matrix was converted to a DESeq2 object using ‘DESeq2::DESeqDataSetFromTximport’  function with design : ~ group, where the group represent the combination of Genotype (i.e. WT for rs736631C/CD226-307Gly protective allele or SNP for rs736631T/CD226-307Ser risk allele) and treatment time (i.e. WT.0h, SNP.0h, WT.4h, SNP.4H, WT.12h, SNP.12h, WT.24h, SNP.24h).
!Sample_data_processing = Raw count matrix was further processed to remove genes that have low / inconsistent gene expression level in samples from the same ‘Group’ (i.e. WT.0h, WT.4h, WT.12h, WT.24h,  SNP.0h, SNP.4h, SNP.12h, SNP.24h). Raw counts were normalized using DESeq2::counts function and genes having less than 10 normalized counts in all groups in 2 of the 10 samples from the same group were filtered out yielding a normalized count matrix with 14965 genes.
!Sample_data_processing = Pairwise differential gene expression analyses were performed to compare SNP.0h vs WT.0h; SNP.4h vs WT.4h; SNP.12h vs WT.12h; SNP.24h vs WT.24h; SNP.4h vs SNP.0h; SNP.12h vs SNP.0h; SNP.24h vs SNP.0h; WT.4h vs WT.0h; WT.12h vs WT.0h and WT.24h vs WT.0h groups using DESeq2::results function with options lfcThreshold set to 0 and alpha set to 0.05.
!Sample_data_processing = For each pairwise comparison, DESeq2 results were exported as a tab-delimited file with raw counts, normalized counts and TPMs for all samples being compared.
!Sample_data_processing = Assembly: ENSEMBL GRCh38 (hg38) v104
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes raw counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes DESeq2 normalized counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes Transcript-per-Million (TPMs) counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding sample information
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding Transcript ID to GeneID mapping info (i.e. tx2gene file used to import salmon quantification file in R using Tximport)
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file for raw count matrix
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file for DESeq2 filtered normalized counts matrix
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding Ensembl gene mapping information for annotation of genes in count matrices

!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = FACS-sorted CD3+CD8+ T cells from healthy donors homozygous for either the protective (CD226-307Gly, N = 10) or risk allele (CD226-307Ser, N = 10) cultured in complete medium RPMI and stimulated with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 monoclonal antibodies for 0h, 4h and 24h were subjected to RNA extraction using RNeasy Micro Kit. Paired-end (2x150pb) sequencing libraries were prepared using  Illumina’s Stranded Total RNA prep kit starting from 7,5ng of total RNA using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. Sequencing libraries were indexed using IDT Illumina UDI A Lig 96 indexes 96 Sp1. RNA sequencing was performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina Novaseq 6000 sequencer using Novaseq 6000 S4 1v5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles) yielding between 20 and 40 millions raw paired-end reads per library.

!Sample_data_processing = For differential gene expression analyses, the raw count matrix was converted to a DESeq2 object using ‘DESeq2::DESeqDataSetFromTximport’  function with design : ~ group, where the group represent the combination of Genotype (i.e. WT for rs736631C/CD226-307Gly protective allele or SNP for rs736631T/CD226-307Ser risk allele) and treatment time (i.e. WT.0h, SNP.0h, WT.4h, SNP.4H, WT.12h, SNP.12h, WT.24h, SNP.24h).
!Sample_data_processing = Raw count matrix was further processed to remove genes that have low / inconsistent gene expression level in samples from the same ‘Group’ (i.e. WT.0h, WT.4h, WT.12h, WT.24h,  SNP.0h, SNP.4h, SNP.12h, SNP.24h). Raw counts were normalized using DESeq2::counts function and genes having less than 10 normalized counts in all groups in 2 of the 10 samples from the same group were filtered out yielding a normalized count matrix with 14965 genes.
!Sample_data_processing = Pairwise differential gene expression analyses were performed to compare SNP.0h vs WT.0h; SNP.4h vs WT.4h; SNP.12h vs WT.12h; SNP.24h vs WT.24h; SNP.4h vs SNP.0h; SNP.12h vs SNP.0h; SNP.24h vs SNP.0h; WT.4h vs WT.0h; WT.12h vs WT.0h and WT.24h vs WT.0h groups using DESeq2::results function with options lfcThreshold set to 0 and alpha set to 0.05.
!Sample_data_processing = For each pairwise comparison, DESeq2 results were exported as a tab-delimited file with raw counts, normalized counts and TPMs for all samples being compared.
!Sample_data_processing = Assembly: ENSEMBL GRCh38 (hg38) v104
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes raw counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes DESeq2 normalized counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes Transcript-per-Million (TPMs) counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding sample information
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding Transcript ID to GeneID mapping info (i.e. tx2gene file used to import salmon quantification file in R using Tximport)
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file for raw count matrix
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file for DESeq2 filtered normalized counts matrix
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding Ensembl gene mapping information for annotation of genes in count matrices

^SAMPLE = GSM8241226
!Sample_title = 316.WT.4h
!Sample_geo_accession = GSM8241226
!Sample_status = Public on Sep 13 2024
!Sample_submission_date = Apr 29 2024
!Sample_last_update_date = Sep 13 2024
!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Homo sapiens
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 9606
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cell type: CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: CD226-307Gly
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cd226 allele: rs736631C
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = treatment: anti-CD3/anti-CD28 mAbs
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = time: 4h
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = FACS-sorted CD3+CD8+ T cells from healthy donors homozygous for either the protective (CD226-307Gly, N = 10) or risk allele (CD226-307Ser, N = 10) cultured in complete medium RPMI and stimulated with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 monoclonal antibodies for 0h, 4h and 24h were subjected to RNA extraction using RNeasy Micro Kit. Paired-end (2x150pb) sequencing libraries were prepared using  Illumina’s Stranded Total RNA prep kit starting from 7,5ng of total RNA using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. Sequencing libraries were indexed using IDT Illumina UDI A Lig 96 indexes 96 Sp1. RNA sequencing was performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina Novaseq 6000 sequencer using Novaseq 6000 S4 1v5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles) yielding between 20 and 40 millions raw paired-end reads per library.
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Peripheral blood mononuclear leukocytes (PBMCs) were purified from buffy coat preparations obtained from consenting healthy blood donors. PBMCs were prepared by gradient centrifugation of buffy coats and kept in frozen in liquid nitrogen. Genotyping of the CD226 SNP rs763361 was performed using TaqMan SNP Genotyping assay. rs763361T expressing CD226-307Ser represented the risk allele, whereas rs763361C expressing CD226-307Gly represented the protective allele. Experiments were performed on cells form male donors homozygous for either allele.Frozen PBMCs were thawed, monocytes removed by plastic adherence and CD8 T cells were purified by negative selection. The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = RNA was extracted using RNAeasy Micro Kit (Qiagen) following manufacturer’s instructions.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Libraries were prepared using the Illumina Stranded Total RNA Prep kit starting from 7.5 ng of total RNA and using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. The kit for the index sequences used was IDT Illumina RNA UDI A Lig 96 Indexes 96 Spl. RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.
!Sample_description = WT.4h.316
!Sample_description = raw_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = DESeq2_normalized_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = TPMs.tsv
!Sample_description = SNP.4h.vs.WT.4h.tsv
!Sample_description = WT.4h.vs.WT.0h.tsv
!Sample_description = samples.RDS
!Sample_description = salmon_tx2gene.RDS
!Sample_description = hsapiens_gene_ensembl_v104.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_unfiltered_60664_genes.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_filtered_14965_genes.RDS
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were processed using nf-core/rnaseq pipeline (v3.8.1) on genotoul SLURM cluster using -profile genotoul, --fasta Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.dna.primary_assembly.fa, --gtf Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.104.gtf ,-- aligner star_salmon options.
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were quality controlled using FASTQC (v0.11.9)
!Sample_data_processing = Adapter and quality trimming was performed using Trim Galore! (v3.4)
!Sample_data_processing = Trimmed reads were aligned to reference genome using STAR (v2.7.10a), BAM files were sorted and indexed using samtools (v1.15.1), duplicates were marked using picard MarkDuplicates (v 2.26.10)

!Sample_instrument_model = Illumina NovaSeq 6000
!Sample_library_selection = cDNA
!Sample_library_source = transcriptomic
!Sample_library_strategy = RNA-Seq
!Sample_relation = BioSample: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/biosample/SAMN41114943
!Sample_relation = SRA: https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/sra?term=SRX24396721
!Sample_supplementary_file_1 = NONE
!Sample_series_id = GSE266132
!Sample_data_row_count = 0
^SAMPLE = GSM8241229
!Sample_title = 101.SNP.4h
!Sample_geo_accession = GSM8241229
!Sample_status = Public on Sep 13 2024
!Sample_submission_date = Apr 29 2024
!Sample_last_update_date = Sep 13 2024
!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Homo sapiens
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 9606
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cell type: CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: CD226-307Ser
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cd226 allele: rs736631T
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = treatment: anti-CD3/anti-CD28 mAbs
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = time: 4h
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = FACS-sorted CD3+CD8+ T cells from healthy donors homozygous for either the protective (CD226-307Gly, N = 10) or risk allele (CD226-307Ser, N = 10) cultured in complete medium RPMI and stimulated with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 monoclonal antibodies for 0h, 4h and 24h were subjected to RNA extraction using RNeasy Micro Kit. Paired-end (2x150pb) sequencing libraries were prepared using  Illumina’s Stranded Total RNA prep kit starting from 7,5ng of total RNA using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. Sequencing libraries were indexed using IDT Illumina UDI A Lig 96 indexes 96 Sp1. RNA sequencing was performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina Novaseq 6000 sequencer using Novaseq 6000 S4 1v5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles) yielding between 20 and 40 millions raw paired-end reads per library.
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Peripheral blood mononuclear leukocytes (PBMCs) were purified from buffy coat preparations obtained from consenting healthy blood donors. PBMCs were prepared by gradient centrifugation of buffy coats and kept in frozen in liquid nitrogen. Genotyping of the CD226 SNP rs763361 was performed using TaqMan SNP Genotyping assay. rs763361T expressing CD226-307Ser represented the risk allele, whereas rs763361C expressing CD226-307Gly represented the protective allele. Experiments were performed on cells form male donors homozygous for either allele.Frozen PBMCs were thawed, monocytes removed by plastic adherence and CD8 T cells were purified by negative selection. The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = RNA was extracted using RNAeasy Micro Kit (Qiagen) following manufacturer’s instructions.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Libraries were prepared using the Illumina Stranded Total RNA Prep kit starting from 7.5 ng of total RNA and using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. The kit for the index sequences used was IDT Illumina RNA UDI A Lig 96 Indexes 96 Spl. RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.
!Sample_description = SNP.4h.101
!Sample_description = raw_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = DESeq2_normalized_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = TPMs.tsv
!Sample_description = SNP.4h.vs.WT.4h.tsv
!Sample_description = SNP.4h.vs.SNP.0h.tsv
!Sample_description = samples.RDS
!Sample_description = salmon_tx2gene.RDS
!Sample_description = hsapiens_gene_ensembl_v104.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_unfiltered_60664_genes.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_filtered_14965_genes.RDS
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were processed using nf-core/rnaseq pipeline (v3.8.1) on genotoul SLURM cluster using -profile genotoul, --fasta Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.dna.primary_assembly.fa, --gtf Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.104.gtf ,-- aligner star_salmon options.
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were quality controlled using FASTQC (v0.11.9)
!Sample_data_processing = Adapter and quality trimming was performed using Trim Galore! (v3.4)
!Sample_data_processing = Trimmed reads were aligned to reference genome using STAR (v2.7.10a), BAM files were sorted and indexed using samtools (v1.15.1), duplicates were marked using picard MarkDuplicates (v 2.26.10)
!Sample_data_processing = Pseudo-alignment and quantification were performed using salmon (v 1.5.2)
!Sample_data_processing = Salmon transcripts quantification files (quant.sf) were imported into R/Rstudio using R ‘tximport’ package and a file linking Transcript ID to Gene ID (‘tx2gene’ file) generated by the nf-core/rnaseq pipeline to produce the raw counts matrix. TPMs values were retrieved from the abundance slot of tximport generated list.
!Sample_data_processing = For differential gene expression analyses, the raw count matrix was converted to a DESeq2 object using ‘DESeq2::DESeqDataSetFromTximport’  function with design : ~ group, where the group represent the combination of Genotype (i.e. WT for rs736631C/CD226-307Gly protective allele or SNP for rs736631T/CD226-307Ser risk allele) and treatment time (i.e. WT.0h, SNP.0h, WT.4h, SNP.4H, WT.12h, SNP.12h, WT.24h, SNP.24h).
!Sample_data_processing = Raw count matrix was further processed to remove genes that have low / inconsistent gene expression level in samples from the same ‘Group’ (i.e. WT.0h, WT.4h, WT.12h, WT.24h,  SNP.0h, SNP.4h, SNP.12h, SNP.24h). Raw counts were normalized using DESeq2::counts function and genes having less than 10 normalized counts in all groups in 2 of the 10 samples from the same group were filtered out yielding a normalized count matrix with 14965 genes.
!Sample_data_processing = Pairwise differential gene expression analyses were performed to compare SNP.0h vs WT.0h; SNP.4h vs WT.4h; SNP.12h vs WT.12h; SNP.24h vs WT.24h; SNP.4h vs SNP.0h; SNP.12h vs SNP.0h; SNP.24h vs SNP.0h; WT.4h vs WT.0h; WT.12h vs WT.0h and WT.24h vs WT.0h groups using DESeq2::results function with options lfcThreshold set to 0 and alpha set to 0.05.
!Sample_data_processing = For each pairwise comparison, DESeq2 results were exported as a tab-delimited file with raw counts, normalized counts and TPMs for all samples being compared.
!Sample_data_processing = Assembly: ENSEMBL GRCh38 (hg38) v104
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes raw counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes DESeq2 normalized counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes Transcript-per-Million (TPMs) counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding sample information
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding Transcript ID to GeneID mapping info (i.e. tx2gene file used to import salmon quantification file in R using Tximport)
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file for raw count matrix
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file for DESeq2 filtered normalized counts matrix
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding Ensembl gene mapping information for annotation of genes in count matrices

RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.

!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were processed using nf-core/rnaseq pipeline (v3.8.1) on genotoul SLURM cluster using -profile genotoul, --fasta Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.dna.primary_assembly.fa, --gtf Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.104.gtf ,-- aligner star_salmon options.
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were quality controlled using FASTQC (v0.11.9)
!Sample_data_processing = Adapter and quality trimming was performed using Trim Galore! (v3.4)
!Sample_data_processing = Trimmed reads were aligned to reference genome using STAR (v2.7.10a), BAM files were sorted and indexed using samtools (v1.15.1), duplicates were marked using picard MarkDuplicates (v 2.26.10)
!Sample_data_processing = Pseudo-alignment and quantification were performed using salmon (v 1.5.2)
!Sample_data_processing = Salmon transcripts quantification files (quant.sf) were imported into R/Rstudio using R ‘tximport’ package and a file linking Transcript ID to Gene ID (‘tx2gene’ file) generated by the nf-core/rnaseq pipeline to produce the raw counts matrix. TPMs values were retrieved from the abundance slot of tximport generated list.
!Sample_data_processing = For differential gene expression analyses, the raw count matrix was converted to a DESeq2 object using ‘DESeq2::DESeqDataSetFromTximport’  function with design : ~ group, where the group represent the combination of Genotype (i.e. WT for rs736631C/CD226-307Gly protective allele or SNP for rs736631T/CD226-307Ser risk allele) and treatment time (i.e. WT.0h, SNP.0h, WT.4h, SNP.4H, WT.12h, SNP.12h, WT.24h, SNP.24h).
!Sample_data_processing = Raw count matrix was further processed to remove genes that have low / inconsistent gene expression level in samples from the same ‘Group’ (i.e. WT.0h, WT.4h, WT.12h, WT.24h,  SNP.0h, SNP.4h, SNP.12h, SNP.24h). Raw counts were normalized using DESeq2::counts function and genes having less than 10 normalized counts in all groups in 2 of the 10 samples from the same group were filtered out yielding a normalized count matrix with 14965 genes.
!Sample_data_processing = Pairwise differential gene expression analyses were performed to compare SNP.0h vs WT.0h; SNP.4h vs WT.4h; SNP.12h vs WT.12h; SNP.24h vs WT.24h; SNP.4h vs SNP.0h; SNP.12h vs SNP.0h; SNP.24h vs SNP.0h; WT.4h vs WT.0h; WT.12h vs WT.0h and WT.24h vs WT.0h groups using DESeq2::results function with options lfcThreshold set to 0 and alpha set to 0.05.
!Sample_data_processing = For each pairwise comparison, DESeq2 results were exported as a tab-delimited file with raw counts, normalized counts and TPMs for all samples being compared.

!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Homo sapiens
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 9606
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cell type: CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: CD226-307Ser
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cd226 allele: rs736631T
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = treatment: anti-CD3/anti-CD28 mAbs
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = time: 4h
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = FACS-sorted CD3+CD8+ T cells from healthy donors homozygous for either the protective (CD226-307Gly, N = 10) or risk allele (CD226-307Ser, N = 10) cultured in complete medium RPMI and stimulated with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 monoclonal antibodies for 0h, 4h and 24h were subjected to RNA extraction using RNeasy Micro Kit. Paired-end (2x150pb) sequencing libraries were prepared using  Illumina’s Stranded Total RNA prep kit starting from 7,5ng of total RNA using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. Sequencing libraries were indexed using IDT Illumina UDI A Lig 96 indexes 96 Sp1. RNA sequencing was performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina Novaseq 6000 sequencer using Novaseq 6000 S4 1v5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles) yielding between 20 and 40 millions raw paired-end reads per library.
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Peripheral blood mononuclear leukocytes (PBMCs) were purified from buffy coat preparations obtained from consenting healthy blood donors. PBMCs were prepared by gradient centrifugation of buffy coats and kept in frozen in liquid nitrogen. Genotyping of the CD226 SNP rs763361 was performed using TaqMan SNP Genotyping assay. rs763361T expressing CD226-307Ser represented the risk allele, whereas rs763361C expressing CD226-307Gly represented the protective allele. Experiments were performed on cells form male donors homozygous for either allele.Frozen PBMCs were thawed, monocytes removed by plastic adherence and CD8 T cells were purified by negative selection. The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = RNA was extracted using RNAeasy Micro Kit (Qiagen) following manufacturer’s instructions.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Libraries were prepared using the Illumina Stranded Total RNA Prep kit starting from 7.5 ng of total RNA and using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. The kit for the index sequences used was IDT Illumina RNA UDI A Lig 96 Indexes 96 Spl. RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.

!Sample_data_processing = Pairwise differential gene expression analyses were performed to compare SNP.0h vs WT.0h; SNP.4h vs WT.4h; SNP.12h vs WT.12h; SNP.24h vs WT.24h; SNP.4h vs SNP.0h; SNP.12h vs SNP.0h; SNP.24h vs SNP.0h; WT.4h vs WT.0h; WT.12h vs WT.0h and WT.24h vs WT.0h groups using DESeq2::results function with options lfcThreshold set to 0 and alpha set to 0.05.
!Sample_data_processing = For each pairwise comparison, DESeq2 results were exported as a tab-delimited file with raw counts, normalized counts and TPMs for all samples being compared.

!Sample_source_name_ch1 = CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Homo sapiens
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 9606
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cell type: CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: CD226-307Ser
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cd226 allele: rs736631T
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = treatment: anti-CD3/anti-CD28 mAbs
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = time: 4h
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = FACS-sorted CD3+CD8+ T cells from healthy donors homozygous for either the protective (CD226-307Gly, N = 10) or risk allele (CD226-307Ser, N = 10) cultured in complete medium RPMI and stimulated with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 monoclonal antibodies for 0h, 4h and 24h were subjected to RNA extraction using RNeasy Micro Kit. Paired-end (2x150pb) sequencing libraries were prepared using  Illumina’s Stranded Total RNA prep kit starting from 7,5ng of total RNA using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. Sequencing libraries were indexed using IDT Illumina UDI A Lig 96 indexes 96 Sp1. RNA sequencing was performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina Novaseq 6000 sequencer using Novaseq 6000 S4 1v5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles) yielding between 20 and 40 millions raw paired-end reads per library.
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Peripheral blood mononuclear leukocytes (PBMCs) were purified from buffy coat preparations obtained from consenting healthy blood donors. PBMCs were prepared by gradient centrifugation of buffy coats and kept in frozen in liquid nitrogen. Genotyping of the CD226 SNP rs763361 was performed using TaqMan SNP Genotyping assay. rs763361T expressing CD226-307Ser represented the risk allele, whereas rs763361C expressing CD226-307Gly represented the protective allele. Experiments were performed on cells form male donors homozygous for either allele.Frozen PBMCs were thawed, monocytes removed by plastic adherence and CD8 T cells were purified by negative selection. The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = RNA was extracted using RNAeasy Micro Kit (Qiagen) following manufacturer’s instructions.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Libraries were prepared using the Illumina Stranded Total RNA Prep kit starting from 7.5 ng of total RNA and using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. The kit for the index sequences used was IDT Illumina RNA UDI A Lig 96 Indexes 96 Spl. RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.

!Sample_data_processing = Pseudo-alignment and quantification were performed using salmon (v 1.5.2)

TPMs values were retrieved from the abundance slot of tximport generated list.
!Sample_data_processing = For differential gene expression analyses, the raw count matrix was converted to a DESeq2 object using ‘DESeq2::DESeqDataSetFromTximport’  function with design : ~ group, where the group represent the combination of Genotype (i.e. WT for rs736631C/CD226-307Gly protective allele or SNP for rs736631T/CD226-307Ser risk allele) and treatment time (i.e. WT.0h, SNP.0h, WT.4h, SNP.4H, WT.12h, SNP.12h, WT.24h, SNP.24h).

!Sample_type = SRA
!Sample_channel_count = 1
!Sample_source_name_ch1 = CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_organism_ch1 = Homo sapiens
!Sample_taxid_ch1 = 9606
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cell type: CD3+CD8+ CD8 T cells
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = genotype: CD226-307Gly
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = cd226 allele: rs736631C
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = treatment: anti-CD3/anti-CD28 mAbs
!Sample_characteristics_ch1 = time: 12h
!Sample_treatment_protocol_ch1 = FACS-sorted CD3+CD8+ T cells from healthy donors homozygous for either the protective (CD226-307Gly, N = 10) or risk allele (CD226-307Ser, N = 10) cultured in complete medium RPMI and stimulated with anti-CD3 and anti-CD28 monoclonal antibodies for 0h, 4h and 24h were subjected to RNA extraction using RNeasy Micro Kit. Paired-end (2x150pb) sequencing libraries were prepared using  Illumina’s Stranded Total RNA prep kit starting from 7,5ng of total RNA using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. Sequencing libraries were indexed using IDT Illumina UDI A Lig 96 indexes 96 Sp1. RNA sequencing was performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina Novaseq 6000 sequencer using Novaseq 6000 S4 1v5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles) yielding between 20 and 40 millions raw paired-end reads per library.
!Sample_growth_protocol_ch1 = Peripheral blood mononuclear leukocytes (PBMCs) were purified from buffy coat preparations obtained from consenting healthy blood donors. PBMCs were prepared by gradient centrifugation of buffy coats and kept in frozen in liquid nitrogen. Genotyping of the CD226 SNP rs763361 was performed using TaqMan SNP Genotyping assay. rs763361T expressing CD226-307Ser represented the risk allele, whereas rs763361C expressing CD226-307Gly represented the protective allele. Experiments were performed on cells form male donors homozygous for either allele.Frozen PBMCs were thawed, monocytes removed by plastic adherence and CD8 T cells were purified by negative selection. The percentage of residual CD4 T cells after depletion was always  less than 0.5% and the remaining populations consisted of 95-98% CD8 T cells.
!Sample_molecule_ch1 = total RNA
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = RNA was extracted using RNAeasy Micro Kit (Qiagen) following manufacturer’s instructions.
!Sample_extract_protocol_ch1 = Libraries were prepared using the Illumina Stranded Total RNA Prep kit starting from 7.5 ng of total RNA and using 14 PCR cycles for library amplification. The kit for the index sequences used was IDT Illumina RNA UDI A Lig 96 Indexes 96 Spl. RNA sequencing was  performed on one S4 lane on an Illumina NovaSeq 6000 sequencer, using NovaSeq 6000 S4 v1.5 Reagent Kit (300 cycles), and a paired-end 2 x 150 pb strategy. Between 20 and 44 million paired-end raw reads were produced per library.
!Sample_description = WT.12h.91
!Sample_description = raw_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = DESeq2_normalized_counts.tsv
!Sample_description = TPMs.tsv
!Sample_description = SNP.12h.vs.WT.12h.tsv
!Sample_description = WT.12h.vs.WT.0h.tsv
!Sample_description = samples.RDS
!Sample_description = salmon_tx2gene.RDS
!Sample_description = hsapiens_gene_ensembl_v104.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_unfiltered_60664_genes.RDS
!Sample_description = deseq2_filtered_14965_genes.RDS
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were processed using nf-core/rnaseq pipeline (v3.8.1) on genotoul SLURM cluster using -profile genotoul, --fasta Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.dna.primary_assembly.fa, --gtf Homo_sapiens.GRCh38.104.gtf ,-- aligner star_salmon options.
!Sample_data_processing = Raw sequencing FASTQ files were quality controlled using FASTQC (v0.11.9)
!Sample_data_processing = Adapter and quality trimming was performed using Trim Galore! (v3.4)
!Sample_data_processing = Trimmed reads were aligned to reference genome using STAR (v2.7.10a), BAM files were sorted and indexed using samtools (v1.15.1), duplicates were marked using picard MarkDuplicates (v 2.26.10)
!Sample_data_processing = Pseudo-alignment and quantification were performed using salmon (v 1.5.2)
!Sample_data_processing = Salmon transcripts quantification files (quant.sf) were imported into R/Rstudio using R ‘tximport’ package and a file linking Transcript ID to Gene ID (‘tx2gene’ file) generated by the nf-core/rnaseq pipeline to produce the raw counts matrix. TPMs values were retrieved from the abundance slot of tximport generated list.
!Sample_data_processing = For differential gene expression analyses, the raw count matrix was converted to a DESeq2 object using ‘DESeq2::DESeqDataSetFromTximport’  function with design : ~ group, where the group represent the combination of Genotype (i.e. WT for rs736631C/CD226-307Gly protective allele or SNP for rs736631T/CD226-307Ser risk allele) and treatment time (i.e. WT.0h, SNP.0h, WT.4h, SNP.4H, WT.12h, SNP.12h, WT.24h, SNP.24h).
!Sample_data_processing = Raw count matrix was further processed to remove genes that have low / inconsistent gene expression level in samples from the same ‘Group’ (i.e. WT.0h, WT.4h, WT.12h, WT.24h,  SNP.0h, SNP.4h, SNP.12h, SNP.24h). Raw counts were normalized using DESeq2::counts function and genes having less than 10 normalized counts in all groups in 2 of the 10 samples from the same group were filtered out yielding a normalized count matrix with 14965 genes.
!Sample_data_processing = Pairwise differential gene expression analyses were performed to compare SNP.0h vs WT.0h; SNP.4h vs WT.4h; SNP.12h vs WT.12h; SNP.24h vs WT.24h; SNP.4h vs SNP.0h; SNP.12h vs SNP.0h; SNP.24h vs SNP.0h; WT.4h vs WT.0h; WT.12h vs WT.0h and WT.24h vs WT.0h groups using DESeq2::results function with options lfcThreshold set to 0 and alpha set to 0.05.
!Sample_data_processing = For each pairwise comparison, DESeq2 results were exported as a tab-delimited file with raw counts, normalized counts and TPMs for all samples being compared.
!Sample_data_processing = Assembly: ENSEMBL GRCh38 (hg38) v104
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes raw counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes DESeq2 normalized counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: Tab-delimited text file includes Transcript-per-Million (TPMs) counts for each sample
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding sample information
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding Transcript ID to GeneID mapping info (i.e. tx2gene file used to import salmon quantification file in R using Tximport)
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file for raw count matrix
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file for DESeq2 filtered normalized counts matrix
!Sample_data_processing = Supplementary files format and content: RDS file holding Ensembl gene mapping information for annotation of genes in count matrices
